In the legislative proposal we have included that the storage period in the future is possible for two years and due up to a maximum of six years. Why is this important and necessary due to the present danger situation? – Of course, we also have to count on sleepers that can only strike at a later date, and otherwise we are at risk of deleting this data too early. But I believe that by this law we have really achieved the goal – balance between freedom and security – to guarantee.

ParlaMint-AT_2015-04-23-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00070_d7e8925	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! I would like to comment on the government paper on occupational safety. At present, about 3.5 million people in Austria are in employment, and more workers than one believes are at least occasionally dealing with dangerous substances. The range of data subjects is relatively large. These are not only the adjacent areas, such as in the chemical industry or in laboratories, but also highly affected, for example, cleaning staff, or are also hair coloring hairdressers, but they are also employees in car workshops, autopainting plants that handle paints and paints. These are just a few examples. The handling of such dangerous, harmful substances is regulated by the Workers' Protection Act, so that no serious health consequences arise from dealing with dangerous substances. In the novelle that is available today, it is mainly about the labelling of dangerous substances, so everyone who has to do with it knows: I must be careful that nothing happens to me – for example, if something is spilled, something is inhaled et cetera. I am not satisfied with this novel, or I am skeptical of some regulatory points. What exactly is it about? – For example, there is a need for labelling only if – quote – nothing stands against it. What, for example, could resist labelling? – This is called: if the container makes the label impossible or makes it difficult, for example because it is a pipe. It is really difficult to understand why it should not always be possible to write on what substance this is and how dangerous it is for the worker or the employee. The second point I have concerns is that the spaces in which such dangerous substances are stored must only be marked if: because there are considerable quantities stored within it. What is a significant amount in detail, but is not regulated in the law. Moreover, the problem is not that someone who picks up something every day may not know what is inside and what to look for, but the problem arises when someone comes in who is not involved in dealing with this substance, as the law demands. The opinion of the Chamber of Labour on this novelle says that this may be emergency services, the fire brigade, the rescue if something happens inside. They need to know that they're entering a space where dangerous substances are stored. I say that it can also be external cleaning staff who had to enter it suddenly, that can be a craftsman waiting for some installation inside, the light et cetera. In so far as I think it is necessary that spaces in which hazardous substances are stored are generally recognizable as such. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] The third criticism is the long transitional period. For ten years, the old labelling system is still to be valid and upright. This means that in these ten years one has either the old marking or the new marking or both parallel, and this certainly leads to confusion. And confusion in this context always means danger to workers. That is why I am simply not satisfied with these ten years. Ladies and gentlemen, I know that some of the points I criticise here are also regulated and concreteized in some regulations, but the problem is that these regulations do not exist to us. They have not yet been negotiated in large part. If, therefore, the law shows missing levels of protection which may be defined later in the regulation, we do not know today at which level the social partners will agree. This is just too little for me when it comes to responsibility – to responsibility for the health and life of workers and workers. Unfortunately, I am not satisfied with the regulations. One point I would like to address briefly is the handling of lead. In principle, young people and women up to now are completely excluded from handling with lead because lead is simply highly toxic and carcinogenic, can affect fertility and can have extremely negative consequences for the embryo in pregnancy. The ban on young people is now slightly changed in the law. However, I was assured by the Ministry that an absolute ban on young people will continue to exist, although in turn postponed into the regulation. But this is different for women – not by this novel, but by something you decided yesterday, hidden in the withdrawal of a reservation for a UN convention. So far, women were 100 percent protected from getting in touch with lead in their work. The air limit value for men will now also be valid for women. Only when lead is demonstrated in the body will a higher level of protection for women become effective again, and I do not think that is acceptable. Yes, lead is also harmful to men, carcinogenic, highly toxic. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] But I simply cannot accept the need to reduce the level of protection for women, and that is why the Greens voted against this point as the only one. Ladies and gentlemen, as has been said, we Greens are not willing to make compromises on the health protection of workers, and on Half of us cannot accept this novel, which certainly also has positive aspects. It's not good enough for us.

ParlaMint-AT_2015-04-23-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00070_d7e9974	Mr President. Mr. Federal Minister! We are now discussing some very heterogeneous agenda items, I am going to speak on item 10 on the agenda, on Mr Loacker's request for a reduction in the Chamber of Labour. Before I do this, I would like to warmly welcome a delegation of the citizens' initiative “High-water-safe Wörgl”. – Welcome. [[General condolences.]] The current request is about the gradual reduction of the Chamber of Workers' housing. This proposal has been rejected in the Committee on Labour and Social Affairs, which I am inclined to say, as expected. The NEOS and the Freedom Party voted in favour. On the occasion of the discussion on this request, a number of interesting figures have also been put forward, which I would like to briefly address. Chamber of Labour: The increase rates in recent years are not insignificant, we are talking about approximately €400 million of forced contributions. The corresponding example, so to speak in the black half of the empire, is the Chamber of Commerce with economic chamber contributions of about €800 million. It is always the same with these institutions, which I would like to call a so-called protected area, because they enjoy the special protection of the legislator, which is even constitutionally protected in the specific case. You remember: In 2007, compulsory membership in the Constitutional Chambers was incorporated with the corresponding fixed revenue for these institutions. We are always meeting in the same areas, which is also reflected in an answer to a question. It is always about special pensions, reserves, provisions at a considerable level, and so it is on this point. In the Chamber of Labour, it is striking that the personal provisions for special pensions, discharges and cetera have increased by 91 percent in the last ten years and amount to around € 130 million. The general free reserves have also increased sharply in the last ten years and amount to €111 million, i.e. the money that these parallel kingdoms can put aside as a reserve. At the same time, in the general budget, we no longer know where to go about loud debts. That is, in this protected area one has the opportunity to put money on the side What's the performance now? – As I said, in the case of special pensions and reserves, this is always a question of the input output relationship. We are – and we say that again and again – not convinced both for the Chamber of Commerce and for the Chamber of Workers that there is a healthy relationship between what it costs companies and what it brings them. – I deliberately say companies and not entrepreneurs, because the service provider and the servicer are sitting together in the same boat in which holes are drilled constantly by this black and red government. We complain – or you complain – that economic growth in Austria has now come to an end, so to speak, that the advance of this boat has come to an end. It's clear: Those who sit in the boat, namely the private sector, are exclusively busy drawing the water out of the boat, and can no longer take care of giving the boat a drive. A topic in this area The area is also that a lot of money is always spent on campaigns – in the Chamber of Workers, for example, and here I quote Franz Gosch, a Christian trade unionist from Styria, who said before the election of the Chamber of Workers elections: "For such campaigns, hundreds of thousands of euros will be wasted on compulsory contributions from Styrian workers. This money is drawn up hard by the employees month after month. The FSG – the Group of Socialist Trade Unions – “will pay their advertising from their own pocket. The AK’s funds shall be used for its members and not for officials.’; [[It was in favour of the FPÖ as well as of the representatives of the NEOS.]] As has been said, we have, of course, agreed to this request, since we also believe that this would have been a good signal for the discharge of companies. We are talking about additional labour costs, where we are unfortunately European champions. To reduce the Chamber of Workers' contributions would have meant a relief from companies to reduce even the Chamber of Commerce' contributions would have been a good signal in the context of the tax reform. What are your signals for the company in the framework of the tax reform? – You increase the capital income tax, you extend the depreciation period, you increase privileged sales tax areas, you increase exceptionally the maximum contribution basis for social security, and you delete the education premium. What kind of signals are these? – This is certainly not a signal that will help our entrepreneurs to succeed in these difficult times in the future. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] We all know that nothing will change, ÖVP and SPÖ will do nothing to change this state. Why not? – These institutions are, of course, the cornerstones of their power, and this is about retaining power. I can only say clearly or clearly: If the FPÖ were in power, we would stop many holes. In this area, I can say that we would massively reduce chamber contributions, because that would be a discharge for companies. We would, of course, seek to abolish compulsory membership in the medium term. Why? – Because the systemically would exert a positive pressure on the chambers to show more efficiency and effectiveness!

ParlaMint-AT_2015-04-23-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00070_d7e10596	Mr President. Federal Minister! Appreciate High House! I would like to refer briefly to item 9 of the agenda, the NEOS request, which concerns the separate identification of the Chamber of Workers' charge on the payroll bill. I would like to make it clear that social democracy and therefore also the SPÖ are always for transparency, and we would like to see this transparency again on the pay bill. [[Abg. New builder: Like the black files!]] [[Abg. New builder: The black files are very transparent!]] The competent ministry is the Federal Ministry of Finance and therefore the allocation to the Financial Committee is provided here. On the agenda item 10, which has been referred to several times: The demand for a reduction in the Chamber of Labour, the contribution to the legal representation of workers, has already been curing for some time. And it is significant that this always comes from the same groups, which make almost an attack on these successful workers' chambers in Austria. [[Abg. Neubauer: What does “attack” mean? How do you define “attack ”?]] And if we look at this, on the one hand, the revenues of the Chambers of Workers from the levy, which is regulated by law with 0.5 percent of gross income, on the other hand, but also the output – that is, the benefits these Chambers of Workers provide for the employees in Austria – we can find, and this is confirmed by the independent, that on 1 € Chamber of Labour comes an equivalent of 3 €. I believe that this clearly shows that the benefits of the Chambers of Workers make the contributions more than double their return to the workers. The performance report – because transparency is always called for here too – clearly shows – and I may be able to confirm this by an example of the Chamber of Labour of Lower Austria, where the annual report is published annually and can also be consulted by the members and the control bodies: The Chambers of Workers make a significant contribution. Especially in times like these, in which we celebrate 70 years of founding of the Second Republic, we are reminded: It was also the Chambers of Workers who, due to the social partners, were substantially involved in the Joint Committee that this country could be built up in such a way. Dear ladies and gentlemen! Since the time is getting a little short, it may only be added: Two requests from the NEOS have been submitted to me, and one refers to examining the legal professional representation and its effectiveness from the Court of Auditors. The Workers' Chamber Act provides exactly for the purpose for which the Chamber of Workers' Circumstances may be used: The Chamber of Labour has the task of representing and promoting the economic, professional, social and cultural interests of the staff. How it represents and promotes these interests falls within the scope of self-government and is not a matter of the supervisory body, the Court of Auditors – and I would like to add that it has already been decided in 1996 that the Court of Auditors may examine the Chambers of Workers and legal representatives and that this examination could be carried out by law from 1997 onwards. The second request is aimed at the abolition of compulsory membership. Dear ladies and gentlemen! We already know this polemic enough. We know very well that there is no compulsion in the Chambers of Workers, but a compulsory membership. And where is the difference? – The difference is that persons who, due to a mandatory membership or non-compulsory membership, can also be released from the Chamber of Workers' Circumstances. These are especially those people who have significant influence on the management of a company – they are not members of the Chamber of Workers despite their independent status. I believe that this also shows a little of the polemic content of some of the statements that have been repeated in the debates on this subject. You will understand that we reject such requests when they are made. However, we would also like to thank the workers' chambers, the self-administrative bodies, the officials who really do great work every day, and especially the workers of the Chambers of Workers who are confronted with the problems of the employees on the spot – and these are not very few in these times, I know from my own work – for their activity and their use. Thank you.

I would now like to comment on my request, namely the request to the Federal Minister, to be so kind and, above all, to ensure that the implementation of the Working Time Directive for doctors in Vienna also takes place or does not lead to disadvantages for patients. We have passed this law. That was a unanimous decision in the National Council in autumn, if I remember quite well. We were still very critical in the Committee, not at all because of the content, but because of the process, because the whole thing did not come about a government proposal, but it was an initiative by the two government parties. At the time, we criticised this insofar as the EU's call for this law to be implemented had been known for ten years. Five or twelve then comes quickly an initiative by the Federal Government, without anyone having the opportunity to deliver an opinion here. That was an interesting discussion, I would say no evil, but a very lively discussion. In the course of this, the Federal Minister said that we could all be assured that all stakeholders and all the countries were on board. What happened after that was that there have been problems in two to three federal states over and over again. Until the end, Vienna is left. This is particularly pious because it is your home country, Mr. Federal Minister. The question arises why it works so badly in Vienna. In an interview, the Vienna Health Councillor said that she was, as it were, surprised that this was now being implemented, and that Vienna had no idea at all. The health city councillor says literally in an “curier” interview: “We need to implement a doctor’s working time law, which the National Council adopted in autumn on the basis of EU regulations. Whether it was useful for him to allow only a period of six weeks for implementation is another question.” – So obviously the deadline was too short. She goes on to say: "This EU directive has not been implemented for ten years. The fact that the Federation is now suddenly implementing it was not predictable for Vienna." Either she's asleep, or that's an excuse she just hasn't done. I don't want to appreciate it any more now. There have been no more incidents there for so long. In addition, we must ask ourselves: What is a pain center compared to a pain clinic? How many such facilities do we need? And: What exactly does it need in addition to the settled area? For example, in my community there is a established pain therapy center, which I believe works very well, but again only for the orthopaedic area. I am glad that my request has been converted into a common request and is also adopted, at least as regards this one point. The GOG is commissioned to establish federal quality standards for improving the care of pain patients, which hopefully happens soon. In this regard, Mrs Oberhauser acknowledged the need for follow-up and welcomed the need to consider whether cannabis drugs should also be included in pain therapy. The second point I would like to address concerns the statistics on the psychotherapy of GÖG/ÖBIG. These statistics have been published every two years since 1992. We know that some 900 000 people in Austria use health care due to mental problems. 840 000 people take psychopharmaca, but only very few then receive qualified psychiatric and psychotherapeutic care, also because of the fact that psychotherapeutic care - in my opinion unlawful - has been quotaed and privatized. This is always due to massive criticism and has drawn our attention to the fact that we have an extreme shortage of care in this area. How does Austria react? – With the typical Austrian solution, the “head-in-the-sand” policy: What I don't know doesn't make me hot, so we're setting up the collection of administrative data. That's exactly what happened. Since 2011, there have been no more such statistics. So we don't know how many people are in psychotherapy at all or using psychotherapeutic medicine. This is an intolerable state. I would also like to thank the Minister who has promised to re-order these statistics from 2016 onwards. Another request that we have made is that the new medical education, as it is currently in the draft, should be better illustrated by Konnex for health reform. We have heard before that hospital hospitals should be relieved and there should increasingly be primary care centres. There are first pilot projects to this end. These primary supply centres depend on the fact that different professional groups and different disciplines work together. However, I believe that this requires quite certain conditions in training. In addition, binding cooperation models are needed so that such cooperation can really work. Dr. Rasinger said in the Health Committee that, in his opinion, the cooperation of doctors and other health professionals works quite well. I think that this cooperation works only partially and, above all, not bindingly. I see again and again – and we also know from the statistics – that people with mental problems are still being sent to the circle for five years, for example, until they come to the right treatment. Even in children with development residues, often many years pass before they receive all therapies and land with the right people. Finally, I would like to say something on a subject on which a nameable vote will then be held, namely Mr Franz's request for the definition of embryos from fertilization. There was also a longer discussion in the Committee. I believe that this is an ethical-religious-philosophical question which cannot be answered here in Parliament. I would also like to say that, with your attitude, Mr Franz, you are far from a possible social consensus and should therefore not be surprised that such requests are always rejected. Thank you.

As you know, in order to meet the necessary savings potential and objectives of €200 million per year, it was important to put in place appropriate structural measures. ==References====External links== On 1 December 2014 the Council agreed on an agreement at government level. The Council of Ministers decided to do so on 20 July. January 2015. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen. The main points of the agreement on the ÖBH 2018 structural adjustment package are as follows: The Federal Ministry of State Defense and Sport receives additional budget resources of € 616 million to meet the urgently needed investment needs, of which € 350 million was set as special funding by 2019 and the rest as a financing commitment from 2020 onwards. This appropriation is intended to cover the necessary investments in air forces, to improve mobility and protection of the force, to implement the military reform and to strengthen the militia. The pilot defense battalion 3 in Salzburg and the Miliz supply battalion are dissolved. The tank battalion 33 is converted into a hunter battalion. Further structural adjustments are made by streamlining the structures of all associations. Furthermore, the reduction of heavy weapons must continue. At least 40 pieces of “Leopard” 2A4 and 42 pieces of M-109 remain in operation. Similarly, the planned structural changes require an adjustment of the military use of military sites. In logistics and in the training organisation, the structures are adapted to the reduced needs. In the ministry or at the commanders and the offices generally 15 percentage points are reduced. Ladies and gentlemen! High house! I am convinced that a joint effort will successfully complete the implementation of this package by 2017. I would also like to address the current developments in the budgetary framework, since these are the subject of the current deliberations. The current proposal, which basically follows a cost-reduction path, has succeeded in achieving a trend reverse after more than a decade in the national defence budget, an increase over the years 2015 to 2019. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen. This development is also due to you, who have tirelessly committed themselves to the Austrian federal army. – Thank you very much for that.

Otherwise, poultry producers would not be able to comply with the legal provisions. Consequently, no further steps have been taken in connection with the currently existing compromise on stockpiling density and on profit orientation, but also on the already high Austrian animal welfare standards at legal level – and we should mention that Austria already has the highest standard in the field of stockpiling density in Europe. We shall therefore reject this motion for a resolution. It is far more important to pursue and continue to work on other objectives, such as reducing antibiotics, combating salmonella, finding new animal welfare indicators. It was therefore agreed, among other things, that the Federal Ministry of Health should start discussions with the poultry industry and the food trade in this regard. A new basis for a native poultry label is to be created and implemented in order to put quality before quantity. And I think this serves in the final consequence animal and human beings in Austria.

ParlaMint-AT_2015-04-23-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00070_d7e18212	Ladies and gentlemen! Today's debate shows an encouraging further development: I can remember that for years there has been no discussion about the execution of the sentence, but a standstill and something like ignoring problems. Today, no one goes to the speaker's desk and says that in the execution of the sentence, everything is fine, rather everyone and everyone says that there is an urgent need for reform and it is important that these reforms take place. This becomes visible only if you look at the implementation of measures and its historical development. When the instrument “action ” was created for mentally impaired perpetrators, about 200 perpetrators were in prison in 1980. Meanwhile, the number is 800. Why has this number so increased? – Because, on the one hand, there are more and more orders and, on the other hand, fewer and fewer redundancies or more often stopped. There are persons who receive seven, eight, nine months of punishment, but are two, three years beyond this punishment in action. On average, each convicted person is in enforcement for two years longer than the original criminal measure to which he was sentenced. The idea was actually to release even faster due to special care. You're far away from that, that's an absolute illusion. The consequences of this development are that, of course, costs are rising and that fewer and fewer resources are available for important care and that the action of its original task cannot be fulfilled at all. The debate on origin provided for a therapist to come to 15 to 20 inmates, i.e. a psychologist, or a doctor. From such a care relationship we are light years away. This means that people are always in the process of taking action for longer and the therapy starts later and later. I'm writing people out of action who say: We don't understand, we're in prison, but the therapy doesn't start, although we're in action and there's actually the idea that the therapy is being started relatively quickly. For some, therapy only starts when the actual sentence is over and the detention begins beyond the penalty. [[Abg. Listen: Most of them! Almost everyone!]] The next problem concerns the assessors of the discharge reports. That's what it says, among other things: ‘Moreover, at least half of the opinions have been set up to a small extent.’ In other words, the same information is repeated many times. Only one third of the experts have a neutral attitude. Neutrality actually belongs to the basic setting of an expert. In some of the opinions, statements on psychological phenomena are made without based them on a single psychometric test. In the study, 40 percent of the opinions are considered insufficient or insufficient. This result is disillusioning, in a double sense, because we could have a wrong negative forecast or false positive prognosis. In the case of the wrong positive prognosis, someone could be released from the implementation of measures that should not actually be released. This would be bad because of course this could lead to crimes that should be prevented. But just as problematic is a false negative prognosis, because it means that someone who could be released from prison after his punishment is stopped. This is, of course, an unsustainable condition under human law. Therefore, the issue of the quality of the opinions is a very central question if we look at the problem of the implementation of measures. One final point on the implementation of measures is the question of follow-up. There is also the usual federal problem. The Penalty is financed by the federal government, the post-care of the countries. Therefore, there is a lack of follow-up. We also need to solve this, because we could release people into post-care, in which they get a therapeutic setting that already allows them to be in freedom. Now we have a very good report from an expert group, which we share and also support in terms of content and which contains very concrete reform proposals, which would further develop the quality of the action. [[I should like to congratulate the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats) and European Democrats.]] The judgment of the German Federal Constitutional Court has clearly stated that the so-called distance offer is a fundamental command. That's four years ago, and since then we haven't done anything in Austria. The legal situation is quite similar in both countries. The German Federal Constitutional Court has found that the safeguard custody in Germany is unconstitutional and has set up some parameters on how to redesign it. These parameters also apply to Austria, but we have not implemented them. The essential reason why these new parameters are needed is that the measure is such a heavy intervention into a fundamental right, namely the fundamental right of freedom, and we can bring people in, and that for life, unlimited. They can be in action for a lifetime. This is a very massive one, one of the most serious interventions in personal freedom. The distance bid, which is a fundamental parameter, states that there must be a clear separation between the execution of penalties and the execution of measures. This clear separation is meant not only in the local sense, but also in the qualitative sense. It is exactly what does not always work in Austria. In various execution facilities, this distance offer is not clearly implemented. Furthermore, it is also clear, and the German Federal Constitutional Court has also stated that the Ultima ratio principle, which is generally to be preceded by criminal law, must also be preceded in this case. If we currently have just under 900 residents in Austria in the implementation of measures, I am very doubted whether the Ultima ratio principle is applied here. I wonder if there would be no relief, so that these people could go on therapy and one could see that these sick people – because these people are usually treated accordingly. In addition, there is the minimization bid, which means the continuous easing of the freedom-restriction measures, the motivating command – that is, that one also motivates people to accept the appropriate therapy – and the individualization bid – that means that one explicitly addresses the people. Legal protection must also be extended accordingly and a support offer must be available. At the moment, it is true that lawyers at hearings are not necessarily allowed, but that this is rather rare. A very essential point is also the control offer, which is becoming more stringent, the longer a person is in the measure. This means that every time you check whether a person could be released from the measure, it would be more stringent than that. criteria apply, for example, that the person must be presented to a judge. That is exactly what is being observed in Austria. You see, we have a very massive human rights problem there, and that has been for years. Yes, you are perfectly right, the request is not, as has been said, the best we have ever discussed here, but it creates consciousness. And the Minister of Justice has made a confession that he wants to change something – now you have to do it again. It is clear to me that this also has something to do with budgetary resources, but it is a massive human rights problem that we in Austria have not long done something in this area to give these people, these sick people - because they are usually - really the help they need.

How am I supposed to explain to a consumer that one AMA seal for meat was fed 30 percent genetic engineering soya from Brazil or Argentina or the USA and the other free from genetic engineering soya, possibly from the Danube Soya project, from our region, from Central Europe? How am I supposed to explain this to a consumer: once genetic engineering in the feed and once free from genetic engineering? – This is a contradiction, Mr Federal Minister, which you need to eliminate urgently and forcefully. This is a possibility that the Commission is considering here. I think you have to act now, at least in the voluntary field, to implement freedom of genetic engineering at 100 percent, because the AMA seal is not mandatory; the AMA seal is nevertheless for products that are free of genetic engineering. That's an hour's commandment, and I'm asking you to do that today. We will closely monitor whether you are taking these necessary and correct steps, otherwise the consumer deception will be. And there we will no longer see – no longer, Minister of State! From this point of view, I now call on the AMA Supervisory Board to really carry out its supervisory function. Because if meat is currently being applied for at a 50 percent discount in the food trade with the award of the AMA seal – ladies and gentlemen – ladies and gentlemen! – then this is ultimately also fraud against farmers and consumers. Then it goes to the ministries where the section heads deal with it. Ministers are dealing with this. This means that all relevant decision-makers have dealt with the subject, and it has been made public. This is the minimum promise we make. Donate.]] 99.9 percent of all hunters do not need a fist firearm for the pursuit of the hunt. A few professional hunters carry a fist firearm – in case of the need for a catch in an injured game. The proponents of this petition want to achieve with terrifying arguments that access to fist firearms is much easier for everyone and anyone. Ladies and gentlemen! We in Austria do not need to arm citizens. In Austria, our excellent executive ensures safety! And that's good.

We have recently done a lot in this area; the care allowance has been created, which is constantly being valorised, 2014 has been introduced in care care care and hospice care, and the care reform 2011 and 2012 has led to the creation of a care fund to ensure long-term care for a long time. With 2016, the care allowance will be increased by 2 percent at all stages. This is, of course, a necessary factor. It is noted that in recent years demographic development has increased costs in the care sector. However, the costs have not only increased, but also the benefits have been further expanded, as I have already pointed out. Currently, 3 billion € are spent on the care system, and 450 000 people receive care money. Care has also become an important economic factor in our country. When I take Lower Austria, 17 500 people work in the care and in the health sector, which is more than 10 percent. We also increase the care in the sense that we raise up first and pay attention to what is needed. Thus, the care of the home and the stationary care are further planned and expanded. In this year, €6 million will be used to favour investment in order to take account of the economic factor. The care system is something particularly valuable, and I say it has improved a lot, but we are constantly on it and it must be our goal to develop it even better so that we can maintain this good standard.

ParlaMint-AT_2015-04-23-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00070_d7e22675	(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Ladies and gentlemen. I refer to the Citizens’ Initiative “Improvement of apprenticeship training”. This citizens' initiative was discussed in the Committee on Petitions and Citizens' Initiatives and passed on by unanimous decision to the relevant Economic Committee. At the first moment one could question the sense of meaning, because the citizens' initiative was introduced by young people who are involved in apprenticeship training in tourism and gastronomy. This is an area that is truly not very simple and easy. That is why the commitment of young people is all the more worthy. More than 4,000 signatures supported the citizens' initiative. Most of the population hardly expect any personal commitment from young people, but they are happy that apprenticeship training will soon end. But this citizens' initiative is showing the opposite. The initiators have become active. A few weeks ago, there was an apprenticeship parliament here in Parliament for the first time, in which representatives of this citizens' initiative were active. What's this about? – Two, three hard facts about this: The young people have said – listen and marvel – that vocational training should be extended from eight weeks to twelve weeks per year of vocational training, that there should be no more than seven hours of education per day and that the careers should become international. So one can say with fug and right, the young people are involved, are motivated and – I may announce – they are on the right path. I hope that this citizens' initiative will be dealt with accordingly in the Committee on Economic and Monetary Affairs and Industrial Policy, according to the inputs. – Thank you for your attention.
ParlaMint-AT_2015-04-23-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00070_d7e22708	Mr Magnificent came to speak. It's a kilnauer. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2015-04-23-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00070_d7e22730	Mr President. (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Values Advisors! At the last meeting of the Committee on Petitions and Citizens' Initiatives, we again had a comprehensive agenda and a wide range of topics to deal with. I will deal with the 380-kilovolt line that crosses about 39 communities from Elixhausen to Kaprun in Salzburg. There are, of course, many different interests in such a large project. On the one hand, the power grid operator and the stability of the power grid are there because the project of the loop closure of a 380 kilovolt line has been pursued for a long time in order to ensure the stability of the power grid. On the other hand, there are, of course, citizens who are concerned about the environmental compatibility of the project submitted and about nature conservation. It is all the more understandable when many of the people concerned express their opinion by supporting this petition. In this case, two opinions were received – from the Ministry of Economic Affairs and the Ministry of Life. Thus, in these cases, the law has a so-called Environmental impact assessment. In this procedure, all relevant legislation on materials will be applied and an examination of whether a project can be approved and then implemented or whether various environmental regulations can be violated and can therefore not be approved. Just such an EIA procedure is currently pending with the Salzburger Landesregierung as the competent authority. It was not until recently that an oral debate took place. I can now very well understand the fears and fears of the citizens, but it would be massively contrary to the principles of the rule of law if senior authorities were to intervene in a ongoing procedure. However, it is also the responsibility of the legislator to consider whether legislative changes are necessary. In this particular case, this has not been the result of the opinions in the context of the petition or the opinions of the two ministries. That is why we have taken note of this petition and not assigned it to a specialist committee. Another word on the use made by Mr Pirklhuber and Mr Höbart. I have already somehow the impression that these Usances, which are practiced in the Committee on Petitions, are very much followed if they meet the intentions of the opposition, but not if they meet the intentions of the government groups. In that sense, I wish you a good night.
